UDC-22-00430
February 6, 2023 
2)
The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat re-arranges lots for multifamily development. The existing lots have received 
conditional use approval and could each be developed with multifamily buildings. The proposed 
plat allows a Greenway to run through the area and the multifamily to be more logically laid out. 
The size and location of lots is similar to those found in the area. The plat appears to be able to 
be served in a logical manner by utilities. The surrounding developable area is largely already 
developed. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facilities and Fees (4.2): 
The proposed subdivision is in an area with existing facilities. The City owns land within the 
plat boundary which can be developed with a public facility (the plan is to utilize the land for 
a Greenway). Park Fees are not charged with the signing of the plat if the plat does not 
increase area, number of lots or development area. As the developable area is being 
swapped with the City. No fees are due at plat signing. 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has largely been previously 
developed. The plat is limited by the railroad to the south. An exception for block sizes must 
be granted. 
 
 
Open Space (4.4): 
The Greenway is part of this plat, and multifamily units may provide open space at the site 
plan level, should the Greenway not meet requirements. 
  
Required Improvements (4.5): 
The streets in the area are existing, but improvements and utilities may need to be 
developed with the plat. 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees will be due with building permits of new construction on the site. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with an exception. 
  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No phasing is specifically proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 

UDC-22-00430
February 6, 2023 
5)
The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage:
A drainage worksheet was submitted and reviewed. Staff will require a drainage study with 
future development of the site. A drainage study will be required with the site plan submittal.  
 
Traffic: 
A traffic worksheet was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with traffic at the plat 
level for the project as presented.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed plat proposes multiple lots for future multifamily development. These lots are 
being adjusted to meet a specific development pattern.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The plat proposes unbuildable lots in the form of common lots for the apartment development 
and a Greenway lot. The City will own the Greenway lot. The common lots are proposed as 
being owned by an undivided interest with maintenance shared by the lot owners. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
The plat is adjacent to developed property to the north. To the south is the railroad track. The 
narrow frontages to the east and west abut largely vacant land. The land has already received 
conditional use approval for multifamily development and could develop in that manner without 
this platting action. The plat allows for a more logical alignment of the project and interface with 
a Greenway. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00430
February 6, 2023 
9)
The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9)
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat.
 
Review criterion not applicable. 
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. As requested by Staff, the entire City-owned parcel is included with the final Plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommended 
changes, clarifications, and corrections for technical issues. Many of the issues have been 
addressed with a revised submittal. Some technical correction still must be addressed, and a 
revised map will need to be submitted prior to Governing Body approval.   
 
The application complies with conditions for this review criterion.   
 
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
 

UDC-22-00430
February 6, 2023 
STAFF REVIEW: 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Correct ownership. 
2. Technical corrections. 
3. Drainage requirements needing to be addressed. 
4. Including the entire City-owned parcel in the plat. 
5. Putting all parking in a common lot. 
6. Note language concerns. 
Most issues have been addressed through a revised submittal. Technical corrections still need to 
be addressed and Note 6 should be removed from the Plat. The utility easements should be a 
general utility easement and not granted to a specific utility company. The easement on the 
original plat was not granted to a specific utility company and should not be changed with this 
Plat.    
 
The applicant has indicated that an easement will be placed on the common parking lot at a 
later date because the parking lot design/location could change during the site plan process. 
Staff is satisfied with this.   
 
CONCLUSION: 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant, with conditions and the granting of an 
exception to block size requirements. The area this plat is proposed in an area with existing street 
network. The property is constrained by the railroad. 
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body. (Staff 
Recommendation) 
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 

UDC-22-00430
February 6, 2023 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body, with the following exception and conditions: 
1. Exception to UDC 4.3.3.c, regarding minimum block size and connectivity for Block 1. 
2. Prior to approval of the final plat the following shall be required: 
a. Technical corrections to the plat and supporting documents shall me made to the 
satisfaction of the City prior to approval by the Governing Body.  
b. Note 6 shall be removed from the Plat.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-07_TotalExtraction_15343671.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE WILL OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY RECOGNIZING THE NEED TO PROVIDE CITY BOARDS, 
COMMISSIONS, AND COUNCILS WITH THE TOOLS THEY NEED TO 
BE EFFECTIVE, EFFICIENT, AND TRANSPARENT." 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne relies heavily on boards, commissions, and councils to 
carry out work on behalf of the city; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne is fortunate to have numerous community volunteers 
who donate many hours of their time carrying out the goals of the boards, commissions, and 
councils that they serve on; and 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body seeks to set clear expectations and standards of conduct 
for when board, commission, and council members engage in business on behalf of the City of 
Cheyenne. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that through the issuance of this Resolution, the Governing Body 
is setting the expectation that any meeting of a City of Cheyenne board, commission or council 
shall be properly noticed with the meeting date, time, and location available on the City website; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that meetings of the City of Cheyenne boards, 
commissions, and councils shall be at locations that are readily accessible by members of the public 
and at which sufficient room exists for members of the public to attend and feel welcome, and if 
held virtually on-line, that the meeting shall ensure that the electronic platform used is readily 
accessible to, and useable by, members of the public; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that all City of Cheyenne boards, commissions, and 
councils shall adopt Robert’s Rules of Order or other standing rules under which to conduct 
business, which rules shall be available for inspection by the public upon request; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that any policies, procedures or rules of a City of 
Cheyenne board, commission or council shall be reviewed by the board, commission or council 
on an annual basis and updated when appropriate and necessary. All policies, procedures and rules 
shall be filed with the Laramie County clerk and the city clerk and posted on the City website page 
designated for the specific board, commission or council or in the same manner as the meeting 
notices are posted; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the minutes of any City of Cheyenne board, 
commission or council meeting shall be kept and provided to the City Webmaster to be posted on 
the City website page designated for the specific board, commission or council or in the same 
manner as the meeting notice was posted prior to the next regularly scheduled meeting; 
 
8:58 am, Feb 08, 2023

BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that all City of Cheyenne boards, commissions, and 
councils shall restrict use of executive sessions during meetings to those topics specifically 
authorized under Wyoming law; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that all City of Cheyenne boards, commissions, and 
councils shall follow the requirements of Wyoming's Public Meetings Act; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that an appropriate training will be provided to all City of 
Cheyenne board, commission, and council members which training shall instruct the members on 
parliamentary procedure, applicable statutory and city code requirements and other matters as may 
be necessary as determined by the office of the city attorney; 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that all City of Cheyenne board, commission, and council 
members shall review and complete a conflict of interest form each year, which form shall be on 
file with the city clerk; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that this resolution shall be effective upon passage and 
execution by the mayor and city clerk. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED THIS _______ DAY OF __________________, 
2023. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST:  
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk

HUMAN RESOURCES DEPARTMENT
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Front Lobby
Cheyenne, WY 82001
(307) 637-6340
MEMO
To:
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council
From:
Darrin Hass
— Director of Human Resources
Subject:
Alternative Funding Program
Date:
February 13, 2023
Project Description:
1.
This program will heap create a partnership with Novobenefits and Paydhealth in which they can
work with our staff who have extremely high prescription costs. This will reduce the employee
out of pocket costs of their RX charges as well as reduce the city’s overall costs of the BCBS
health plan.

Approved to
form only:
Date: 2(lD/
RESOLUTION NO.
ENTITLED: “A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING PAYDHEALTH, LLC, TO PROVIDE
ADVOCACY AND FINANCIAL CASE MANAGEMENT SERVICES FOR
ELIGIBLE
SPECIALTY
PRESCRIPTION
DRUGS
THROUGH
ALTERNATIVE FUNDING PROGRAMS EFFECTIVE JULY 1, 2023.”
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne wishes to participate in an
alternative funding program through the City of Cheyenne Healthcare Plan (“the Plan”), which
will result in significant prescription drug savings for high-cost specialty medications;
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne will utilize Paydhealth, LLC, to administer the
alternative funding program;
WHEREAS, Paydhealth, LLC, is provider of healthcare benefits consulting services,
which offers funding solutions to the self-funded market for health and welfare benefits to reduce
the costs of healthcare plans.
WHEREAS, the City’s health insurance consultant, Novo Benefits, will oversee the
program with Paydhealth, LLC, and assist eligible employees with using this service;
WHEREAS, the limited number of impacted employees will have individualized
assistance and support with providing needed documentation.
WHEREAS, no expenses will be incurred by the City because Paydhealth LLC’s
associated fees are funded from a percentage of the savings achieved through the Plan under the
alternative funding program;
WHEREAS, it is expected that the City of Cheyenne and its employees will financially
benefit from this program through a reduction of costs for eligible specialty prescription drugs;
and
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne will participate in the program with
Paydhealth, LLC, to provide eligible employees with prescription drug advocacy and financial
case management resources to reduce costs for certain specialty prescriptions; and
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the City’s new alternative funding program, under
which eligible employees may receive program benefits, shall be effective July 1, 2023.
Page 1 of 2

Plan Members Taking Specialty Drugs
1
2
3
Paydhealth will initiate outreach to you by text message or phone call.
Complete the digital enrollment application which will allow
Paydhealth to match you to alternate funding programs.
Note: you may be asked to provide household size and income information.
Your Paydhealth Case Coordinator will coordinate with you and your pharmacy to
ensure you are able to get your medication in a timely manner.
A Case Coordinator is available (8:00 am to 8:00 pm CST) to guide you through
the enrollment process and the program. Please respond to calls from your Case
Coordinator in a timely manner.
This program will not share your information with any 3rd party solicitors. If you would like to
complete your application over the phone or speak with a Paydhealth case coordinator, please call
(877) 869-7772. Common questions and answers about your Plan’s Select Drugs and ProductsTM
Program can be found on the next page.
There are two reasons why you are receiving this important message:
Your Plan has added an important
Your Plan is continuing to offer generous
program that includes the Paydhealth
:.
specialty drug benefits while attempting to
Select Drugs and Products TM List*.
reduce costs to you and the Plan.
*The Paydhealth Select Drugs and Products’ List includes drugs typically prescribed by a specialist for multiple sclerosis, hepatitis C,
Crohn’s disease, hemophilia, cancer, psoriasis, rheumatoid arthritis, transplants, HIV/AIDS, and other complex conditions.
COI)y(i(Jht 2022, Paydhealth, LLC AI ights ssevert

PajdheaIth
I.
I Iiugram
Group Health Plans partner with Paydhealth to offer the Select Drugs and ProductsTM
Program for their Plan participants who are taking certain specialty drugs. The Select Drugs
and ProductsTM Program includes alternate funding and financial case management services
that focus on the economic needs of Plan participants while leveraging clinical services
already included through the Plan’s prescription drug benefit. The Program reduces costs for
Plan participants and may reduce the Plan’s cost, with minimal impact on treatment time.
Key things to know:
Enrollment in the Select Drugs
and ProductsTu Program
provides an opportunity
to substantially reduce a Plan
participant’s specialty drug out-
of-pocket cost — in some
cases, to no cost at all.
A Plan participant must
complete prior authorization
and enroll in the Program to
meet Plan coverage criteria.
Otherwise, the specialty drug
prescription cost will not be
paid by the Plan, and the Plan
participants will pay the full cost
of the specialty drug charged
by the pharmacy.
All specialty drugs on the
Select Drugs and ProductsTM
list require clinical and
administrative review, they
must be medically necessary,
and must be submitted to
alternate funding before a
benefit will be payable.
JW
Copyright 2022, Paydhealth, LLC All rights reserved

Is alternate funding only for people with no healthcare coverage?
No. Many alternate funding programs have been offered in the market for more than twenty
years and the market has steadily grown year over year. Many of these programs are
specifically designed for individuals who have a healthcare coverage. Alternate funding
includes any form of funding provided to a Plan participant outside of a health and welfare
plan.
How will this impact me? What should I expect?
Most Plan participants will see little or no out-of-pocket cost for specialty drugs when they
receive alternate funding. When a specialty drug claim is processed under the prescription
drug benefit of a Plan, it is automatically subject to prior authorization review. This is a
standard procedure that assures the specialty drug is appropriately used and is meeting the
medical necessity requirements of the Plan. When this clinical review is completed, a near
simultaneous administrative review of the alternate funding market is completed.
The Select Drugs and ProductsTM Program is a “chaperone” service. A Program case
coordinator will initially contact the Plan participant to begin the administrative review. This
initial call takes about 10 minutes where a case coordinator will review alternate funding with
the Plan participant and the requirements of identified programs. Some alternate funding
programs require that the Plan participant attest to the accuracy of the information
submitted. The case coordinator will notify the Plan participants if such an attestation is
required. The process routinely takes less than 72 hours when all information is gathered.
How is Paydhealth informed about members who may qualify for
this Program?
Paydhealth is notified of a Plan participant who may qualify for the Select Drugs and
ProductsTM Program through an identification code that is associated with a specialty drug
claim. This notification happens in near real-time and will initiate outreach to the Plan
participants by the Paydhealth case coordination team. The Plan participants will usually
hear from Paydhealth within 24 hours of the pharmacy submitting a claim for a medication
on the Select Drug and ProductsTM list. During this period, the Pharmacy and the Plan
participant’ healthcare provider/prescriber will attempt to confirm the medical necessity for
the prescription under the Plan.
Will my provider need to provide any information to Paydhealth?
The Plan participant’s provider office may need to provide information to Paydhealth and to
any alternate funding programs identified by Paydhealth on the Plan participant’s behalf.
Paydhealth will also work with the Plan participant’s provider office to facilitate any new
prescription requests at the Plan participant’s direction. The Plan participant’s provider may
be asked by an alternate funding program to confirm the Plan participant’s diagnosis.
Copyright 2022, Paydheslth, LLC All rights reserved.

How often is the Select Drugs and Products list updated?
The Program drug list is updated every three months, using a rolling update model. Only
brand-name specialty drugs are included in the Program. Therefore, if the product
becomes available generically, the generic specialty drug benefit will apply.
What about non-compliant members?
Our experience indicates that very few Plan participants will be non-compliant. In these
instances, communication is key. Case coordinators make every effort to engage
non-compliant Plan participants, and if unsuccessful, Paydhealth will reach out to the
Plan for assistance in communicating with a non-compliant Plan participants.
Does the Select Drugs and ProductsTM Program include all
specialty drugs?
While the Program includes the vast majority of specialty drugs, certain categories
are not included in the Select Drugs and ProductsTM Program. These categories include
specialty drugs used in an urgent or emergency care setting that are typically
administered in a healthcare facility. Example categories are allergens and toxins,
viscosupplements, infertility drugs, IUDs, and blood thinners. Also, generic or multi-
source drugs are not included in the Program.
Does the Select Drugs and ProductsTM Program include personal
importation (Canadian Pharmacy) or pharmacy tourism?
No. The Program includes only U.S. pharmacy-supplied specialty drugs. The Program is
designed to manage the funding of specialty drug prescriptions and it does not provide
access to products dispensed by pharmacies or healthcare practitioners located outside
of the United States of America.
Will I be billed for services, and are there fees associated with
the Select Drugs and Products5M Program program?
The only charges that a Plan participant may experience are related to their published
out-of-pocket expense payable to the pharmacy or an alternate funding service charge.
In most cases, the Program will succeed in securing enough alternate funding that a Plan
participant’s out-of-pocket will be lower than their Plan defined co-payment or co
insurance. The Program will never charge a Plan participants directly, and all Program
fees are paid by the Group Health Plan.
Copyrht 2022, Paydheallh, LLC. All rights reserved.

REQUEST:

UDC-22-00365-01
February 6, 2023 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
Move to approve the Hill Heights Addition, 2nd Filing Preliminary Plat, subject to staff conditions. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval  
 
UDC Article 3 Impact Studies 
 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for 0.4 acres of land to create a cottage lot 
residential development. The County Assessor presently shows the land as one ownership parcel.  The land is 
developed with one rural-residential home.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
The property is currently zoned as County AR Agricultural Residential, but is proposed to be zoned NR-3 
Neighborhood Residential – High Density. The NR-3 zone encourages higher-density development in a 
more walkable neighborhood. 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the preliminary plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land has been designated as 
Urban Residential. Surrounding areas are also designated Urban Residential. The Urban 
Residential designation encourages a mix of residential development, and the proposed plat is 
consistent with this expectation.

UDC-22-00365-01
February 6, 2023 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map. 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne support the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision of 
services (including emergency and education services). This development is largely surrounded 
by existing infrastructure. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat is largely surrounded by existing developments. The land to the north and 
west is developed with townhome and duplex style development. The area immediately south 
and east are developed with a more rural residential development pattern. Civic uses are to the 
south across Dell Range. There are constraints that prevent this plat from making any significant 
additions to the street network. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Transportation Network: 
This plat is proposed in an area with a developed network, and there are limited options for the 
project to make any meaningful connections. The existing block is roughly 3,000 linear feet, 
which is 800 feet larger than the requirement. 
 
Community Facilities and Fees: 
The property is not significant in size or scope and there are existing community facilities in the 
area. Staff would not recommend dedicating or reserving land for public parks, schools, fire 
stations, or other similar facilities.  
 
Park Fees are due with this plat. For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to 
the nearest tenth of an acre. The required fees for the .4 acres are as follows: 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$140 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee   
 
 
 
$140 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$280.00 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
 
Public Safety Fees are also due with this development, but these fees are due with building 
permit. The fee is $836.88 in 2021 dollars and adjusted annually. 
 

UDC-22-00365-01
February 6, 2023 
Open Space:
This plat is exempt from being required to provide open space at the plat level. However, the 
development proposal is cottage lots which requires a shared open space with a site plan. The 
plat proposes a common lot to satisfy the open space requirement.  
 
Required Improvements:
This proposed subdivision is in an area with existing infrastructure, and limited additions are 
needed. The applicant should clarify if any utilities are proposed to be extended (information on 
utilities is typically provided with a preliminary plat and was not with this application). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
  
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No phasing is proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
The applicant submitted a drainage worksheet and a traffic worksheet. No other studies were 
provided with the Preliminary Plat application. 
 
Drainage: 
The review of the drainage worksheet submitted with the preliminary plat generally found the 
need for more significant drainage analysis to be done with the project. 
 
Traffic:
The traffic worksheet submitted with the application was deemed acceptable for this level of 
review. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
The proposed development pattern is cottage lots, which allows units to be 50% of the lot 
required lot size in the UDC, so long as the missing area is made up in the common open space. 
The proposed lots are a little short of the required minimum for townhomes, and there is some 
concern the lots might not meet the required side setback when development occurs. The lots 

UDC-22-00365-01
February 6, 2023 
should be modified accordingly, but the common lot is large enough these changes could be 
accommodated.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
The common lot is labeled as “shared” on the plat map, which likely means common ownership 
is proposed; however, this will be required to be better defined with the final plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
Surrounding properties are largely already developed, which include rural residential properties 
which have the potential to redevelop with more urban patterns. Nearby development includes 
duplexes and townhomes, although at a lower density than this project proposes. Less than 
quarter miles away properties are redeveloping with apartments. This plat is an infill project in 
an area with a variety of residential development types. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
Staff is satisfied that the proposed plat is not harmful to the public, health, safety, or general 
welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.    
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder are not proposed with this Preliminary Plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.     
 
 
 
 

UDC-22-00365-01
February 6, 2023 
STAFF REVIEW: 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Lack of defined common lot ownership. 
2. Technical corrections. 
3. Lot dimensional standards (size, setbacks). 
4. Lack of a utility proposal. 
5. Drainage requirements. 
 
CONCLUSION:
This application meets review criteria with some clarifications and corrections. An exception will need to 
be granted due to the constraints of previous development. 
 
ALTERNATIVES: 
1. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions (Staff 
Recommendation). 
3. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the plat does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, findings 
for denial must be stated. 
4. Postpone the preliminary plat until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.  
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add conditions, or 
decide contrary to staff’s recommendation.  In the event conditions are modified, the conditions shall relate to 
applicable review criteria.  In the event that the Commission acts contrary to staff’s recommendation or 
modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons (based on the review criteria) for 
approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval of this application, with the following exceptions and conditions: 
1. The following exceptions to are granted:  
a. Exception to UDC 4.3.3.c, regarding minimum block size and connectivity. 
2. Concurrent with final plat submittal: 
a. Submit a drainage study addressing drainage concerns for this development. 
b. Demonstrate correct utility provision. 
3. Correct the plat as follows: 
a. Increase the size of the townhome lots to meet minimum size requirements. 
b. Remove the notary for City and have the Mayor and Clerk sign the plat. 
c. Specify ownership for the common lot. 
 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Current Zoning Map 
2. Future Land Use Map 
3. Submitted Master Application Form 
4. Applicant-submitted Preliminary Plat

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2023-02-07_TotalExtraction_85371317.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Governing Body, City of Cheyenne 
FROM: 
 
Renee Smith, Grants Manager 
DATE: 
 
FEBRUARY 6, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
GRANT AGREEMENT FOR THE 18TH ST INTERCEPTOR PROJECT 
 
The City of Cheyenne has been awarded a U.S. Department of Homeland Security (DHS) Federal Emergency 
Management Agency (FEMA) FY 2021 Hazard Mitigation Grants Program grant for project scoping of the 
Cheyenne 18th Street Storm Sewer Interceptor Advance Assistance Project. 
The federal award amount is $270,000 with a city match of $30,000 to be funded from the 1% Special Drainage 
Account (5th Penny Tax).  The performance period for the project is from August 5, 2021 through July 31, 
2025.   
The main focus of the project area is the lower/downtown portion of the Capitol Basin and represents the final 
phase required to provide critically needed flood protection and ensure the continued vitality of the 
community’s core business district.  Grant funds will be used for hydrologic and hydraulic evaluations, data 
collection, research, and storm sewer and hydraulic structure design that will be used to draft and submit an 
implementation grant.  
Please let me know if you have any questions or need clarification on this grant agreement.  
Renee Smith 
307-637-6285 
rsmith@cheyennecity.org 
 
cc Thomas Cobb, City Engineer

The City's 18th Street Storm Sewer Interceptor Project is being proposed with a new outlet configuration to take 
advantage of existing city infrastructure. The Cheyenne 18th Street Storm sewer Interceptor Project investment is 
located in downtown Cheyenne, Wyoming and will provide a benefit to many of its citizens and residents of the High 
Plains Economic Development District which includes Laramie, Albany, Goshen and Platte Counties. The investment 
will positively impact the region by improving drainage within downtown Cheyenne and the near west side and will 
protect Cheyenne's core business district from flooding. The proposed flood control infrastructure serves eight of the 
largest 20 employers in the community's business district with 18,000 employees working in it on a daily basis. We 
are pursuing Advance Assistance funding at this time to fully scope and finalize the design, prepare a project BCA for 
pursuit of construction funding, and finalize the design.

HAZARD MITIGATION GRANT PROGRAM *
ADVANCE ASSISTANCE PROJECT APPLICATION 1W 
Provide any relevant information or explanation below. 
The provision of critically needed infrastructure will enhance the environment for economic development and Job 
creation. The main focus of this application is the lower/downtown portion of the Capitol Basin and thi::; project 
represents the final phase required to provide critically-needed flood protection and ensure the continued vitality of 
the community's COr'e business district. The capitol Basin covers approximately 11.7 square miles including the 
downtown business and historic districts, F.E. warren Air Force Base, the State Capitol complex and the City and 
County comple)(es. Additionally, many critical facilltles are rocatect within the rower downtown benefit area 
associated with the 18th Street Project, including the Climb Wyoming Facility, located at 17th Street & Warren; the 
Cheyenne Transitional Center managed by Avalon Corrections, located at 17th Street & Pioneer; the CRMC 
Emergent Care facility, located at 18th Street & Van Lannen; the Union Pacific Rail Yard including their diesel 
pumping area directly south of the historic Union Pacific Depot; Community Action of Laramie County, located at 
17th Street & Capitol: and the new Public Safety Building,'Emergency Management, located at the comer of 18th 
Street & Pioneer. The City of Cheyen ne is part of the current Wyoming Region 7 Multi-Jurisdictional Hatard Mitigation 
Plan. The Plan includes mitigation strategies for flood control projects including those planned for the Lower capital 
Drainage Basin. Our proposed 1su. Street project addresses the following FEMA Community Lifelines; 5afety & 
Security;Community Safety; Shelter; Transportation; and Critical Facilities from life-safety hazards and property 
damages due to flooding. This project addresses the most critical flood risk for the Capitol Drainage Basin.

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
February 8, 2023 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and BenchMark 
Engineers, PC for the Transfer Station Lift Station Upgrade or Replacement Project 
______________________________________________ 
Two (2) Responsive Proposals were submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #4-23, for the 
Transfer Station Lift Station Upgrade or Replacement Project.   
 
The proposals were received from:  
1. BenchMark Engineers, PC.  Cheyenne, Wyoming 
2. Burns & McDonnell.  Centennial, Colorado 
 
Upon review of the two (2) responsive proposal by the evaluation panel, the group has 
recommended acceptance of the proposal submitted by BenchMark Engineers, PC, for a project 
award amount of $88,425.00 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications based review and scoring of proposals:  BenchMark 
Engineers, PC submitted the highest scoring proposal, therefore their sealed pricing envelope was 
opened. 
 
Funding:   
023-14-1417-43-43501 (Solid Waste Fund / Improvements) 
$88,425.00 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-23 
Transfer Station Lift Station Upgrade or Replacement Project  
Page 1 of 7 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
TRANSFER STATION LIFT STATION  
UPGRADE OR REPLACEMENT 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and BenchMark Engineers, PC, located at 1920 Thomes Avenue, Suite 320, 
Cheyenne Wyoming 82001 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the 
date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services for the Transfer Station Lift Station 
Upgrade or Replacement Project, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for 
Proposal (RFP) #4-23, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s 
submitted proposal, incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by October 31, 2023. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount 
of Eighty-Eight Thousand, Four Hundred Twenty-Five Dollars ($88,425.00) as stated 
in Exhibit B – Price Proposal.  
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
 
3:22 pm, Jan 30 2023

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-23 
Transfer Station Lift Station Upgrade or Replacement Project  
Page 2 of 7 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-
consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees or sub-
consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 4-23

Page 3 of 7 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement.

If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10.

With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or

b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to October 31, 2023.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17.

the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have

venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming.

immunity.

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
BETWEEN 
THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND ARTS CHEYENNE 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (City), located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, Arts Cheyenne, a Wyoming nonprofit corporation, P.O. Box 
247, Cheyenne, WY  82003, hereinafter known as the “Organization.”  The City and the Organization are 
collectively referred to as “Party” or “Parties.” 
WHEREAS, the City recognizes that art, including public art, is important to Cheyenne’s 
quality of life.  This recognition is embodied in Title 12, Sections 12.24 of the Cheyenne 
Municipal Code.  The City also recognizes that it can assist in adding value to the community 
through the acquisition, exhibition, and maintenance of public art; 
 
WHEREAS, through ordinance, the City has established the Art in Public Places Program 
to: 
1. 
Enrich the public environment for both residents and visitors;  
2. 
Increase public access to works of art and promote understanding and awareness 
of the visual arts in the public environment;  
3. 
Promote a variety of artistic expressions in the community;  
4. 
Contribute to the community's civic pride in its cultural diversity;  
5. 
Support visual art; 
WHEREAS, the Organization’s purpose is to promote creativity and artistic expression in 
the community.  The Organization recognizes that art, including public art, can represent the 
community’s cultural persona and improve Cheyenne’s livability; 
WHEREAS, the City and the Organization are devoted to improving the community 
12:46 pm, Feb 06 2023

 
Page 2 of  10
through public art initiatives, cultural planning, and cultural programming. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is to renew cooperative interaction between 
the City and the Organization, to define strategic and tactical responsibilities for art-based 
initiatives between the Parties, and to continue endorsement of the Organization as the official 
Local Arts Agency for the City. 
 
2. 
Term.  This MOU shall commence on the date last executed by the duly 
authorized representatives of the parties to this MOU and shall remain in full force until 
terminated as provided herein. 
 
3. 
Payments.  Neither Party shall have any obligation to make any payment of any 
kind to the other Party under this MOU (or if payment, describe terms of payment and method). 
 
4. 
Responsibilities of the Organization.  The Organization shall: 
a. 
The Organization shall designate and manage a Public Art Committee as 
defined by Cheyenne Municipal Code Title 12, Chapter 12.24.  As such, it shall: 
1. 
Fulfill the goals set out in Section 12.24.010 of Municipal Code, as 
outlined above; 
2. 
Recommend potential purchase of works of art to the City of 
Cheyenne governing body for final approval; 
3. 
Develop, execute, and maintain a community-wide public art 
policy for the City of Cheyenne; 
4. 
Seek matching funds, donations, and grants to assist in the 
operation of the Art in Public Places program; 

 
Page 3 of  10
b. 
The Organization shall develop and present a community-wide public art 
policy to the City of Cheyenne governing body by a date mutually agreed to by the 
Parties.  As such it shall: 
1.   
Manage an asset inventory of existing public art in the community; 
2. 
Continue to evaluate comparable public art policies in similar 
communities; 
3. 
Develop model agreements to manage public art design, 
installations, maintenance, and liability. 
c. 
The Organization shall serve as the City’s official Local Arts Agency, 
providing arts-related services and programs for the benefit of the community as a whole. 
 
5. 
Responsibilities of the City. The City shall: 
a. 
The City shall continue endorsement of the Organization as Cheyenne’s 
official Local Arts Agency.  As such, it will: 
 
1.   
Renew through a Resolution through the Governing Body that the 
Organization is Cheyenne’s official Local Arts Agency, serving as the Art 
in Public Places Committee, providing arts-related support, services, and 
programming for the benefit of the community as a whole. 
 
2. 
Include the Organization in community development planning 
activities, meetings and decisions; 
3.  
Designate a City governing body representative to serve on the 
Organization’s Board of Directors; 
b. 
The City may confer Art in Public Places related appropriations to the 

 
Page 4 of  10
Organization to be held in a restricted account for public art purposes.  The amount of 
such transfer shall be determined by Resolution of the Governing Body. (Addendum A). 
c. 
The City will provide annual appropriation funding to the Organization, 
upon submission of an appropriate budget request and updated contractual documents, to 
support arts and culture programs and initiatives that enhance the community and its 
quality of life. (Addendum A) 
d. 
The City shall provide a contact within the Community Recreation and 
Events Department to coordinate with the Organization for assistance as required, 
including but not limited to developing guidelines for projects, submission of work 
products, and implementation of the requirements set forth in this MOU. 
 
6. 
General Provisions.  
 
a.

this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the Parties to this MOU shall be

 e.

ADDENDUM A 
As of this year, Arts Cheyenne identified more than 400 existing public art pieces 
and installations in Cheyenne and Laramie County, including the Big Boots, 
bronzes, murals, utility boxes, and other works of art.  These pieces include those 
owned by the City of Cheyenne, by quasi-governmental organizations, and by 
private businesses in public settings.  
About 50% of those installations have been catalogued and stored in the 
Cheyenne/Laramie County Public Art Inventory database.  The existing Public Art 
Inventory is visible on the Arts Cheyenne website (artscheyenne.com).  The 
remainder of this local public art inventory will be catalogued in FY2022/23. 
Arts Cheyenne and its partnership with other entities creating public art is providing 
a clearer picture of Cheyenne & Laramie County existing public art 
management/maintenance and future installations. 
For the City of Cheyenne’s 2023/24 Fiscal Year, Arts Cheyenne requests a $50,000 
appropriation supporting its public artwork.  This work includes: 
• 
completing the Cheyenne/Laramie County Public Art Inventory 
• 
managing the work of the Public Art Committee to oversee 
Cheyenne/Laramie County public art activities 
• 
fundraising for future public art installations 
This appropriation would be earmarked to the following purposes: 
- 
Management & Public Art Activities - $25,000 (Unrestricted) 
- 
Existing Public Maintenance/Repairs - $10,000 (Restricted to Purpose) 
- 
Support of New Public Art - $15,000 (Restricted to Purpose) 
Arts Cheyenne and the Public Arts Committee are working to expand public art’s 
influence on Cheyenne and Laramie County’s quality-of-life and to enhance its 
attractiveness to residents and visitors.  The City of Cheyenne’s financial support 
will help make that process successful.

Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP 
Subject: Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Goat Systems, LLC, for 
clarification and assurances of City development and reimbursement processes. 
Date: 
February 13, 2023 
 
Background:  
 
The item for consideration is a Memorandum of Understanding between the City of Cheyenne and Goat 
Systems, LLC.  
 
The proposed Governing Body review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral  
2/13/2023 
• 
Public Services Committee  
2/14/2023 
• 
Final consideration 
 
2/27/2023 
 
The developer intends to develop a data center campus on Lot 1, Block 1, High Plains Business Park. The 
developer seeks assurances from the City regarding development processes prior to commencing 
development activity on the property. The intent of this MOU is to affirm the allowance of certain uses 
on the property, summarize applications and further agreements anticipated for the project, and clarify 
when a vested right is established.    
 
Specifically, this MOU clarifies that a data center use and accessory uses, such as storage buildings and 
alternative energy facilities related to the data center campus, are permissible on the LI Light Industrial 
zoned property.  The MOU also details that a vested right to develop in accordance with a Site Plan may 
be established upon approval and subsequent action by the developer within the UDC-specified time 
period, two-years after approval. The MOU also references a future development agreement regarding 
the construction and timing of public improvements associated with the development and the City’s 
Administrative Plat process which could be utilized to add or relocate any easements as utility 
infrastructure is developed. 
 
This agreement expires twenty five years from the date of approval, if the developer does not 
commence development of the project within two years of Site Plan application approval, or if the land 
is transferred to another business entity that is not an affiliate or subsidiary of the Developer, whichever 
is less.  The agreement may be extended to contiguous properties acquired by the developer.   
 
This agreement is not intended to grant the developer any specific permissions or waivers that are not 
commonly available to all developers.   
 
Attachments: 
1. Memorandum of Understanding 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366

BETWEEN CITY OF CHEYENNE, A MUNICIPAL CORPORATION AND

THIS MEMORANDUM OF AGREEMENT (Agreement) made this ______ day of 
___________, 2023, by and between Goat Systems LLC, a limited liability corporation, 
(Developer) and City of Cheyenne, a municipal corporation, (City) provides that: 
 
WHEREAS, Developer is owner of certain real property (Property) in City of Cheyenne 
legally described as Lot 1, Block 1, High Plains Business Park as shown on Exhibit A which is 
attached hereto and incorporated herein; 
 
WHEREAS, Developer proposes to develop on the Property a data center campus and 
ancillary uses in phases over an extended period of time; 
 
WHEREAS, City recognizes the fiscal impact is a benefit to the community; 
 
WHEREAS, Developer seeks assurances from the City regarding development processes;  
 
WHEREAS, a data center campus and ancillary uses are permissible uses in the LI Light 
Industrial zoning district subject to Site Plan approval and compliance with applicable 
development regulations; 
 
WHEREAS, the City does not prohibit the installation of alternative energy facilities 
accessory to a development provided such facilities meet the review criteria for Site Plan approval 
within the specified district; 
 
WHEREAS, the City Site Plan process of Article 2 of the Unified Development Code 
protects the public and neighboring property owners from public health, safety or general welfare 
concerns; 
 
WHEREAS, Developer seeks clarification of the vested right established by approval of a 
Site Plan to aide in assurances to allow the Developer to proceed with a phased development 
approach;  
 
WHEREAS, Developer seeks clarification on participation requirements for public 
improvements and reimbursement of public improvements associated with the Project; 
 
WHEREAS, the City Administrative Plat process of Article 2 of the Unified Development 
Code allows for the administrative modification of easements with the consent of easement 
holders, without public notice or hearing;  
 
WHEREAS, development of the Property requires offsite public improvements such as 
utilities and transportation infrastructure; 
10:57 am, Feb 09 2023

 
Page 2 of 5 
WHEREAS, Developer and City agree that Developer is to have no greater or lesser rights 
or obligations than those available to or imposed upon any other developer in the community under 
the Cheyenne Municipal Code.  This Agreement, the Development Agreement and other 
agreements between the Parties are for purposes of memorializing those rights and obligations 
agreed upon with respect to the Project; 
 
WHEREAS, Developer is eligible to file for reimbursement for offsite public 
improvements in accordance with the provisions of Title 1, Chapter 16 of the Cheyenne Municipal 
Code;  
 
WHEREAS, certain City assurances will allow Developer to develop the Project in 
multiple phases as well as associated public improvements with reasonable expectations; and, 
 
WHEREAS, Developer desires this agreement be allowed to extend to contiguous 
properties acquired by Developer in the future; 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the City and Developer in consideration of the mutual covenants 
and promises set forth herein hereby agree, covenant, and represent as follows: 
1. 
The foregoing recitals are adopted as binding provisions of this Agreement. 
 
2. 
Pursuant to Article 2 of the Unified Development Code, the City shall accept a Site 
Plan application for the Project detailing the first phase of the Project and a less-
detailed general design for subsequent phases.  The City’s approval of said Site 
Plan shall establish a vested right for subsequent phases of the Project to develop 
in accordance with the approval, if the first phase is acted upon within two (2) years 
of Site Plan approval (e.g., commencement of site grading or access road 
construction or design).  
 
3. 
City agrees to work with Developer to execute a Development Agreement without 
delay to detail phasing of public improvements.  
 
4. 
Reimbursement expectations shall be subject to filing of a reimbursement request 
pursuant to the reimbursement provisions of Title 1, Chapter 16 of the Cheyenne 
Municipal Code.  
 
 
 
5. 
The Developer may extend the rights and approvals under this Agreement and the 
Development Agreement to geographical territory acquired by the Developer 
provided the territory is contiguous with the Property or prior extensions. 
Contiguity will not be adversely affected by the existence of a platted street or alley, 
a public or private right-of-way, a public or private transportation right-of-way, a 
lake, stream, reservoir or other natural or artificial waterway located between the 
property and territory sought to be added. Such additional geographical territory 
shall thereafter be included in the agreement. Developer shall provide written notice 
to the City ten (10) days prior to acquisition of any geographical territory. 
 

 
Page 3 of 5 
6. 
Neither this Agreement nor any provision hereof may be changed, waived, 
discharged, or terminated orally and may only be modified or amended by an 
instrument in writing, signed by the parties hereto. 
 
7. 
All rights, powers, and remedies herein given the City are cumulative and not 
alternative, and are in addition to all other statutes or rules of law. Any forbearance 
or delay by the City in exercising the same shall not be deemed to be a waiver 
thereof and the exercise of any right or partial exercise thereof shall not preclude 
the further exercise thereof and the same shall continue in full force and effect until 
specifically waived by an instrument in writing executed by the City. All 
representations, warranties, covenants, and the provisions hereof shall be binding 
upon Developer, its successors and assigns and inure to the benefit of the City, its 
successors, and assigns. 
 
8. 
All notices shall be in writing and shall be deemed to have been sufficiently given 
or served when sent by certified mail when addressed to the parties at the addresses 
set forth below. Such addresses may be changed by notice to the other party given 
in the same manner. 
 
9. 
This Agreement and all covenants, agreements, representations, and warranties 
made herein shall survive the execution of this Agreement and shall continue in full 
force and effect so long as the Agreement is outstanding and not terminated 
pursuant section 10. 
 
10. 
If any provision of this Agreement is held invalid, such invalidity shall not affect 
other provisions of this Agreement which can be given effect without the invalid 
provisions and, to this end, the provisions of this Agreement are hereby declared 
severable. 
 
11. 
This Agreement expires twenty-five (25) years from the date of signing by all 
parties, if the Project does not commence within two (2) years of Site Plan approval, 
or if it transferred into another business entity that is not an affiliate or subsidiary 
of Developer, whichever occurs first. 
 
12. 
No party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or agreements made herein 
without the prior written consent of the non-assigning party, which approval shall 
not be unreasonably withheld. 
13. 
The City does not waive governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement 
and specifically retains all immunities and defenses available to it as a 
governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all other 
applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
 
14. 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be 
governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming 
shall have jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the

parties, and the venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, 
Wyoming. 
 
15. 
Each Party to this Agreement shall assume the risk of any liability arising from its

16. 
Subject to the Parties’ rights and obligations pursuant to the Cheyenne Municipal

represents the entire and integrated agreement between the Parties and supersedes

17.
